aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"268\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"270\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x059c01","title":"Two Aunts between Them","volume":"伙伴外传：The Quiet Seat","anchor":"chapter-0271","paragraphs":["Noor had expected a quiet train journey. Rowan had expected to sit beside her. Neither expectation survived his aunts. Vera held a hardline view of reserved seating; Ona, who read a left-liberal magazine whenever her sister could see its cover, believed spontaneous arrangements were philosophical. They occupied the two seats between the younger travelers and began to argue about orchids.","\"A promising start,\" Rowan said.","\"A bummer,\" Noor answered.","His delight was unmistakable. She had rarely heard him laugh so freely at a word. The train's richly painted compartment promised the atmosphere of the Silk Road, although the actual route lasted less than a day. Historical maps and an outdated menu supplied the theme. Noor wanted companionship, not a lecture about trade monopolies. Rowan wanted the carriage to acquire a vacancy beside her.","Rowan could only marvel at the sufficiency of his aunts' appetite for strife. A majority of passengers were quietly reading. The sisters' wild theories threatened to disturb them. He was reliant on Noor's composure, a selfish hope he did not entirely like. Her own behaviour remained kind, though he suspected her patience had potential limits. The side effect was an immediate improvement in his manners.","Vera's botanical crate occupied the aisle. An inexperienced assistant had loaded it with the wrong side up. Ona criticized the train's attempt to standardize every package, while Vera condemned her sister's misguided indulgence of young workers. Noor asked for the box's actual instructions. Lobby talk about efficient transport did not move a heavy crate. Rowan and a porter did.","The effort required Rowan to hold his nose. Rot in a damaged pot had produced a pronounced smell. The orchids were healthy, the gardener said, but part of their packing had become wet. There was no need to condemn the whole shipment. The smell was upsetting enough to make Ona flee toward the window. Vera intentionally remained still, as if endurance were a contest she could win.","A rustling sound came from behind the crate. Rowan found a young man wedged between luggage. The jobless traveler had boarded illegally after losing his fare money. His name was Eli, and he looked more frightened of Vera than of the conductor. A misguided attempt at self-reliance had led to a situation he could no longer explain elegantly.","Noor asked whether he was hurt. He shook his head. Rowan called the conductor rather than hide him. Eli's survival did not require another lie, and the train staff could decide how to address the fare. The boy's pronounced shiver was psychological as much as physical. An audience of judging strangers would not help.","The conductor moved him to a seat while checking his account. Vera began a hardline speech about poverty and character. Ona countered with a left-liberal account of institutional failure. Eli grimly stared at his hands. Noor stopped the debate. \"He's here. Ask him.\" A short spell of silence followed, broken by a small sob he tried to hide.","The directness hit them both. Their lobby talk had made a real person into a convenient topic. Vera looked out of touch, and Ona looked somewhat ashamed. Rowan admired Noor's refusal to make kindness theatrical. It could transcend a political label without pretending the labels meant nothing. His affection for her had become increasingly difficult to disguise.","Eli described an apprenticeship that had ended when the workshop closed. He had a licence for gardening work, not a job. His mother's diabetes required ordinary ongoing care, and his father had recently died after a heart attack. A bill from his own pneumonia had emptied his savings. He had wanted to reach a cousin who might help him find work. The troubles were real; they did not make boarding illegally wise.","\"In the end, I made it worse,\" he said.","\"You made another problem,\" Noor replied. \"We can discuss this one.\"","The conductor would not disclose private details to the carriage. He arranged a call to the cousin, verified the destination, and explained the legal options. Rowan offered to cover the fare without making repayment a test of gratitude. Eli accepted after a long pause. No one pretended the gift would complete his life. It would get him off this train legally. He could adapt to kindness, however profound the embarrassment.","Vera's sternness began to soften. She had known disease and separation herself; her sister's long illness had once put their household in crisis. Ona had recovered, but their protective habits remained. Their alliance could be generous or suffocating. They were generally better when they remembered the distinction. Noor did not need to diagnose them to see it.","The railway's emphasis on well-being included a portable ramp for a passenger with a disability. The porter moved it into the spacious corner beside a picture of lamps once fueled by gas. The surrounding disorder had not made him forget the person waiting. Noor watched his care with gratitude. He needed no spotlight or celebration to do his work well.","A judicial memoir protruded from Vera's bag beside an invitation to a nephew's ordination. The contradiction amused Rowan. She could analyze a complicated case for hours and still make an immediate judgment about a frightened boy. She caught his look and said, \"That was needless.\"","\"Needlessly harsh,\" Ona corrected.","\"Thank you for improving my apology.\" She disliked a name-and-shame performance. Chivalry was not extinct, but discretion helped it survive.","The uproar over the packing began again when a loose panel fell. Rowan lifted the trim and found a small wrench. The porter had left it after a repair. A vacuum flask rolled across the floor, emptying itself into a harmless puddle. Noor helped the worker make space. The physical mess was easier to solve than the interpersonal one.","The cook arrived with tea. Its boil had steamed the window, and the humidity made Ona complain about her hair. Rowan could not stay indifferent to Noor's laughter. He handed her the first cup and asked the porter to take the lead in clearing the aisle. The man did so efficiently. A young worker's competence had quietly answered a speech about his generation.","At last, Noor found a seat beside Rowan. Their surroundings were somewhat less grand than advertised, but the tea was hot. He looked ready to describe his happiness in detail. She leaned back and told him to stay put. Window","volume":"伙伴外传：The Quiet Seat","anchor":"chapter-0272","paragraphs":["The train stopped beside a wild stretch of coast. A signal fault would delay them, the conductor explained. The aunts' uproar returned at once. Vera wanted a hardline deadline; Ona wanted the company to standardize a clearer announcement. Noor wanted both to stop talking long enough to hear the actual update.","\"A critical distinction,\" Rowan said, then wisely moved his cup before she could spill it on him.","The delay's side effect was a longer lunch. A cook had enough food for everyone if the portions were modest. Eli volunteered to help unload the service trolley. He was inexperienced with its latch and asked for guidance. The porter showed him. The jobless traveler could contribute without pretending a paid fare had made him an employee. Nobody intended to turn help into a new obligation.","Vera inspected a richly painted map of the Silk Road. Its faded caption was outdated, and she wanted the historical guide to correct it. Ona, still in a philosophical mood, said every age revised the route in its imagination. Rowan asked whether either of them could transcend the urge to deliver a lecture before lunch. Their joint look was unmistakable. He had finally united them against a common enemy.","Noor's laugh made him feel sixteen. The psychological effect had become a side effect of every shared outing lately. He was reliant on her interest in a way he did not want to make her responsible for. A selfish impulse might have used the delay to monopolize her attention. Instead, he offered her a quiet walk along the platform and accepted when she wanted to wait with Eli first.","The younger man had begun to learn what had happened to his fare. He had lost his wallet in the station, not been robbed. A call from the lost-property office confirmed it. His original account had been upset by fear, and now he felt the sting of humiliation. He had not intentionally invented a crime, but he had allowed the assumption to grow. Noor asked him to correct it with the conductor.","He did. The staff's account of his boarding illegally would remain accurate. His other claim could be revised. The conductor did not name-and-shame him over the public speaker. Eli's disgrace was already larger in his imagination than the practical incident. A harsh announcement would serve no useful safety purpose.","Eli offered to take the lead in stacking the plates. Vera would not condemn him for an imperfect start. The sting of the earlier exchange had changed her voice. Her grimly dramatic view had proved needless; an anxious young man was not a personal challenge. Rowan asked her not to disturb the cook, who was carrying hot soup. She obeyed, to Ona's open astonishment.","Ona's left-liberal commentary expanded into a criticism of fare structures. Vera countered with a hardline objection to evasion. Eli asked whether they could discuss something other than him. The request was pronounced, courteous, and entirely reasonable. Noor watched the aunts consider it. Their lobby talk had found its limit in the person beneath it.","The crate offered a safer topic. A small insect had crawled from the damaged packing, causing Vera to hold her nose as if it offended every sense at once. The gardener caught it in a jar for identification and kept the plants isolated until he could check them properly. An exotic-looking pest did not become extinct or harmless because he found it attractive. He would follow the actual transport requirements, not the aunts' guesses.","Rowan helped move the box into a spacious luggage corner approved by the staff. The rot had been confined to packing material, which went into a disposal bag. He borrowed the wrench to secure a loose tray, then returned it. The trim around the compartment no longer rattled. A modest repair outweighed several pages of commentary.","Noor asked him whether he always enjoyed fixing other people's mornings. \"Only when it impresses the right person,\" he said.","\"A dangerous admission.\"","\"Legally binding?\"","\"Not in my domain.\"","Her smile hit him harder than the tease. He would happily accept a train journey twice this long if it came with the same seat. The thought was misguided in terms of practical comfort, but his heart had never submitted to a sensible timetable. He wanted to tell her in detail and resisted, for once, the pleasure of being eloquent.","Eli's cousin called again. The gardening work was real, though he could promise only an interview. The uncertainty was upsetting, yet the opportunity outweighed the shame of arriving late. A majority of choices did not offer perfection. Eli accepted the plan, then helped the cook divide the remainder of the meal. His self-reliance now included asking where the plates were.","The vacuum flask had been emptied before the latch broke. Its absence left a void in Vera's picnic arrangements, but she survived. The old biostatistician called it a spell of enforced simplicity. The aunts' strife finally subsided enough for them to look at their surroundings. An hour without being right about something seemed a promising experiment.","The old biostatistician across the aisle watched with interest. He had been silent through the political dispute and now offered Eli a biscuit. His own granddaughter had found her work through an ordinary introduction. A chance could arise from a little companionship without somebody claiming to have revolutionized a life. The comment was neither indifferent nor grand. Noor liked him immediately.","A change in the light alerted Rowan to the sea outside. The wild shore held the remains of an old jetty, its stone steps polished by water. Vera described it as a hazard, which it probably was for anyone foolish enough to leave the platform. Ona wanted to marvel at it instead. The sisters could do both without winning an argument.","Noor joined Rowan by the window. Their reflection stood close together, his shoulder level with the top of her head. The sufficiency of the moment surprised him. He did not need to fill every void with charm. He could simply share a view with a woman who had chosen his company.","\"In the end,\" she said, \"you got your quiet seat.\"","\"And a spectacular journey.\"","\"We haven't moved for an hour.\"","He smiled. \"My definition is flexible.\"","She looked at him, then let her shoulder remain against his. The signal changed, and the carriage began to move."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1065,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0272\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The Quiet Seat</div><h1>A Seat beside the Window</h1><p id=\"chapter-0272-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8616\" data-words=\"8616\">train</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7989\" data-words=\"7989\">stopped</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-928\" data-words=\"928\">beside</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9235\" data-words=\"9235\">wild</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8025\" data-words=\"8025\">stretch</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1547\" data-words=\"1547\">coast</a>. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">A</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7552\" data-words=\"7552\">signal</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3168\" data-words=\"3168\">fault</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9312\" data-words=\"9312\">would</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2206\" data-words=\"2206\">delay</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8426\" data-words=\"8426\">them</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1735\" data-words=\"1735\">conductor</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3036\" data-words=\"3036\">explained</a>. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-654\" data-words=\"654\">aunts</a>&#x27; <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8928\" data-words=\"8928\">uproar</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7078\" data-words=\"7078\">returned</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-607\" data-words=\"607\">at once</a>. Seat","anchor":"chapter-0273","paragraphs":["The train reached the city under a sky the color of polished silver. Rowan should have been eager to leave its cramped grandeur. Instead, he watched Noor gather her bag and felt a faint resentment toward the end of the journey. His aunts were finally quiet. Even their hardline dispute over orchids had exhausted itself. Her departure left a void in the seat, and a new tension in his chest.","Eli met his cousin on the platform. The conductor confirmed the destination before letting him leave, then returned to ordinary duties. The young man had boarded illegally and faced the consequences through the company's process. He did not have to become a public warning. Noor accepted his thanks without offering a philosophical account of his future. \"Good luck at the interview,\" she said.","Vera had written him a practical address for work clothes sold cheaply nearby. Ona supplied a contact for a left-liberal community magazine seeking a gardener for its courtyard. Neither promised a position. Their lobby talk had finally produced something a person could use. Eli could choose what to do with it. He took both slips, smiling for the first time without strain. The sting of his earlier fear had faded, leaving him tired.","The aunts' botanical crate passed inspection after the damaged packing had been removed. Its route and ownership were documented. The gardener explained the requirements in detail, and Vera listened rather than improving them. A misguided attempt to standardize every living thing would not have helped. Clear information about this shipment did. The outdated menu in the carriage could be corrected another day. Their shared impatience had briefly damaged the carriage's environment.","Rowan held his nose as the last bag of sour packing was carried past. \"A memorable bouquet.\"","\"A needlessly vivid one,\" Noor said.","\"We must transcend the memory.\"","\"Please.\"","Her laugh gave the day its proper finish. The physical discomfort had been real, but it did not outweigh the pleasure of her company. He had learned that she could be decisive without making a performance of it. She had seen him accept being told to sit down. These were not insignificant discoveries.","A porter dropped a small parcel near the door. Eli returned to help, intentionally interrupting his own departure to lift it. The worker thanked him, and the exchange ended there. The boy was still jobless, still inexperienced, and still uncertain about the interview. He was also capable of being useful. One description did not consume the rest of him.","Noor watched Rowan watch the exchange. His concern was not entirely unselfish; he liked believing the world could offer second chances. Yet the selfish part did not make the help false. He had wanted to impress her and had still paid a fare without demanding an audience. That was an ordinary kindness she could accept without idealizing him.","The psychological aftertaste of the morning surprised her. She had expected to feel drained by strangers' needs, but the journey had included laughter and an hour of looking at water. Her affection for Rowan was becoming less theoretical. She could marvel at a man's warmth without making herself responsible for managing it. He would have to do some of that himself. Rowan would commit to that Thursday with no need for ceremony.","Vera asked Rowan whether Noor would join them for dinner. He looked at her rather than answering. The small courtesy was unmistakable. Noor declined the family meal, then asked him whether he wanted tea the following week. His expression altered so quickly that Ona laughed aloud. He accepted with admirable brevity.","\"An inexperienced negotiator,\" Vera observed.","\"Entirely reliant on goodwill,\" he replied.","The answer made Noor smile. Their time together would not have to arise from a crisis or an upsetting interruption. They could intentionally meet because they wanted to. Its quietness appealed to her more than a declaration at the station. He seemed to understand that too. His wealth had made generosity easy, but not every kindness simple.","The biostatistician waved from a taxi. His biscuit tin remained open on his lap, ready for the next stranger. Noor asked the driver to help with the heavier bag and received a cheerful nod. The older man's kindness had not required a speech about survival. Sometimes a biscuit was simply a biscuit, and that was sufficient.","A boy running beside the barrier caused an uproar when his hat blew onto the track. Staff stopped him, then arranged retrieval when it was safe. The needless danger ended before it became a fall. Vera began to criticize the child's family, then stopped. The morning had apparently taught her a useful pause. She could take the lead in waiting without becoming the judge of every parent.","Outside, Mara and Cassian stood beside a car, having come to meet them. Mara took one look at Rowan and said, \"A successful journey.\"","\"A philosophical one,\" he answered.","\"That's not what your face says.\"","Noor laughed, and the aunts began speaking at once. Cassian opened the door while Mara made room for the account. The station's noise softened behind them. In the end, there would be tea, a new address, and a young man who might find work. Rowan would also have next Thursday, which made him feel absurdly rich."],"questions":[],"wordCount":881,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0273\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The Quiet Seat</div><h1>Next Thursday</h1><p id=\"chapter-0273-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8616\" data-words=\"8616\">train</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6752\" data-words=\"6752\">reached</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1464\" data-words=\"1464\">city</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8786\" data-words=\"8786\">under</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7627\" data-words=\"7627\">sky</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1589\" data-words=\"1589\">color</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6264\" data-words=\"6264\">polished</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7567\" data-words=\"7567\">silver</a>. He looked at Mara. \"Will that do?\"","The crew applauded. Mara, wearing a synthetic fur cape over her work clothes, tried to remain professional. She had come to restore a photographic backdrop for the old studio's reopening. Somehow she had acquired a standing-room ticket, a costume, and a husband who looked far too pleased with his casting.","The studio had once made industrial films. Its first exhibit followed petroleum from crude oil to finished fuel. A clean empty can bore the word GASOLINE; the invoice beside it called the contents petrol. Chrome fittings shone above an engine cutaway. A zinc plate carried the maker's name, while an ore trolley and a model furnace showed where metal production began. The model's coke was real, though it would never be burned indoors.","Dr Talin Saye, the retired chemist advising the show, was more interested in the food-science film. It concerned acrylamide research and the way a photographic image could render a complicated process intelligible. Noor had come to help with a modern projection about a neurotransmitter. Neither woman wanted their whole careers reduced to serious voices in white coats. Talin accepted a ridiculous crown made from a rubbish bag and laughed at herself in the mirror.","The room's crystal chandeliers had survived decades of neglect. Lumber from a removed set lay stacked beyond a barrier, and the old rubble had been cleared by professionals. A painted tar barrel stood beside a harmless imitation of industrial sludge. Real trash had filled the place when the volunteers arrived. Now Della's costumes and a restored screen gave it another life. The visitors could see the work without wading through junk.","The local labor group had restored the cement path. Garbage no longer blocked the piano, whose old ivory keys needed a musician. Its prominent place beside the screen made Della nervous. A little dye from the costume had left her glove sticky. Mara gave her a discreet solution: exchange hands when posing. The director was lenient about unnatural elegance; laughter might arouse more interest than perfection.","Ivo checked the fog equipment with the technician. Carbon dioxide, nitrogen, and hydrogen appeared in the historical display, but no visitor would handle a gas cylinder. The modern theatrical frost and spray came from supervised equipment. Nobody would rely on an antique machine just because its chrome looked impressive. A small fan carried the fumes from an outside engine away from the open doors. An aluminum star hung from a suspension cable above the artificial ice.","The projected moon was a white crystal shape above an ice-colored landscape. Della's synthetic fur cape made Mara resemble a queen of winter. Wax stars decorated its collar, and a mould on the table showed how they had been formed. The old set's lumber had become mountains in shadow. A little slack in the cloth produced a wrinkle that Mara removed with a careful adjustment.","Talin brought sandwiches and a carbohydrate diagram from the food exhibit, which she had accidentally used as a tray. Noor turned it right side up. A calcium chart was hiding under the plates. \"My students used to say everything became teaching material,\" Talin said.","\"Even lunch?\"","\"Especially lunch.\"","The fat content of a biscuit was not the afternoon's most romantic topic, so Cassian offered Mara an ordinary cup of tea. The liquid warmed her hands. She wanted to stay till the lighting test, then leave before anyone proposed a sequel. Her sense of well-being had become dangerously easy for him to influence. He knew exactly how to make a long working day feel like a private celebration.","Talin had studied at the University of California, Berkeley before she took to museum work. She could speak of acid etching for hours, but refused to make the opening another lecture. The fallout of an old publicity dispute had already taught her to condense a story. Even she could outgrow an affection for a passing fad. An adaptable explanation would survive longer than a rigid slogan. Thus she offered tea instead.","A school display showed a molecule of water in blue and white. Beside it, an old illustrated warning described ozone near certain electrical equipment. Talin had kept the historical text separate from modern operating guidance. The objects were for interpretation. The active lighting rigs belonged to a current technician, who did not need an amateur audience improving his work.","The archive room held celluloid fragments, collodion plates, and a sealed historical thermometer with mercury. Mara would touch none of those while adjusting a backdrop. The conservator moved the real material into controlled storage before visitors entered. Talin could explain the imagery with reproductions. A projection did not need an original fragile object beside a cup of tea.","The film's extract showed a factory worker washing mud from his boots. Cotton fibres clung to his coat. The camera's slow movement gave ordinary labor a dignity the studio's old publicity had missed. Mara watched the figure cross the screen and thought of her mother's hands. No lecture could have made the image more personal. Ivo could generate a fluid movement of light across the old newsprint texture.","The director wanted to publicize the restoration with one brief scene. A queen would cross a moonlit room while a prince offered her a star. Cassian asked whether she would accept it. Mara told him to speak more persuasively. His resulting smile made the director demand silence on set. The amount of stuff required amazed him. A bipedal cardboard robot guarded the tools.","Ivo had volunteered to upskill in digital projection. He could transmit a test image but needed help with the color calibration. An apprentice showed him, and he listened without performing seniority. Talin was equally willing to learn. Over the years, she said, a career became easier when a grown-up could admit a new tool was new.","A fire alarm sounded before the final test. The technician stopped the equipment and directed everyone toward the clear exit. Mara took Cassian's hand. The night might have begun as a romance, but it would not continue on the assumption that a signal was decorative."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1032,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0274\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Paper Moon Studio</div><h1>A Queen for the Lighting Test</h1><p id=\"chapter-0274-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2375\" data-words=\"2375\">director</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-551\" data-words=\"551\">asked</a> Cassian <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8538\" data-words=\"8538\">to</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4940\" data-words=\"4940\">look</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-532\" data-words=\"532\">as if</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3847\" data-words=\"3847\">he</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-184\" data-words=\"184\">adored</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4765\" data-words=\"4765\">leading</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4701\" data-words=\"4701\">lady</a>. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3847\" data-words=\"3847\">He</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4941\" data-words=\"4941\">looked at</a> Mara. &quot;<a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9239\" data-words=\"9239\">Will</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8383\" data-words=\"8383\">that</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2502\" data-words=\"2502\">do</a>?&quot;</p><p id=\"chapter-0274-p2\"><a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2000\" data-words=\"2000\">crew</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-452\" data-words=\"452\">applauded</a>. Staff extinguished the small flame with the appropriate equipment, then kept the room closed for inspection. The electrical rig would not restart until checked. The little incident left an acrid smell but no injured person. Mara held her fur cape around her shoulders and waited beside Cassian. The director could publicize the new outdoor scene instead.","Talin looked at the empty petroleum display through the glass. Its gasoline can and petrol invoice had briefly seemed sinister to a visitor, though neither held fuel. The chrome engine cutaway, zinc label, ore trolley, and coke samples were all inactive exhibits. A vivid picture could arouse a fear far larger than the actual problem. The trained staff checked the real source instead of following the most dramatic theory.","The audience had gathered in a diverse cluster beneath the awning. A boy in an aluminum crown asked whether the moon had burned down. Talin showed him the paper moon on her phone. Its photographic image could transmit a little reassurance while the grown-ups did their work. The same child had loved the food film about acrylamide, mostly because a cartoon potato wore spectacles. Talin accepted that as a beginning of interest. The fallout of the alarm could be managed without blaming a frightened child.","Noor carried out the refreshments with the café staff. A calcium chart remained beneath one tray, and the carbohydrate diagram emerged from under a bowl. The old chemist would not outgrow her habit of turning lunch into an exhibit. \"A fat chance,\" she said when Noor teased her.","The phrase made Mara laugh. Her sense of well-being returned in small pieces: warm tea, Cassian's hand, and the certainty that everyone had left the room. The queen's standing-room ticket had acquired an outdoor performance. The director offered to cancel the promotional scene, but the actors wanted to attempt it when the technician approved a different space.","Della's synthetic cape had lost a wax star. She found it on the clear path and checked the rest. The mould could produce another later; the present garment needed only a minor repair. Ivo held a light while she worked. He had once helped keep a ship moving through a storm. He could now help a designer thread a needle without diminishing either task. Della checked her glove for dye before touching the repaired seam.","The suspension of filming let Talin rescue her notebook. Its crude cover had a molecule drawn by a child. A liquid stain had softened the paper, but the sketches for etching with acid survived. She had accumulated far too much stuff over the years. The camera could transmit an image of the notebook later; she would rely on the conservator to advise her about the actual paper. Mara wanted two things, namely tea and her husband.","Talin's projection showed the restored industrial film against the exterior wall. The scene's rubble, sludge, tar, and lumber belonged to a factory yard long gone. A worker crossed it carrying rubbish to a bin. The new publicity would not render the old place beautiful by cutting all the junk from view. The director chose the whole short extract, including the person cleaning up after the main process. His indispensable helper took to the controls without fuss. The boy put his scraps in the garbage bag.","A volunteer carried a granite bench into a better position with help from three others. A cast iron plaque stood nearby, its surface marked by dew. The studio's garden had once been a dump. Now a biomass project used plant waste in a separate educational area, and the children grew seedlings with compost rather than an unmarked industrial fertilizer. The diverse projects had slowly made a welcome place.","Ivo had planned to upskill indoors. Instead, he learned how to condense a projection for a smaller temporary screen. The old newsprint texture could be retained without forcing every image into a rigid frame. His instructor was lenient about the first crooked attempt. A passing fad would disappear; a skill practised patiently might remain useful. He liked that prospect.","The fog's carbon dioxide system remained off. The historical nitrogen and hydrogen diagrams were still visible through the window, as was the ozone warning. Nothing in that display authorized anyone to operate the current equipment. The outside scene would use moonlight, a lamp approved by the technician, and a cloth backdrop. Talin's crystal pendant could stand in for the star. The technician explained how to extinguish a test lamp safely before the next session.","Mara watched frost-colored light settle across the screen. Her cape's fur caught silver along its edge. A loose strip of celluloid had been reproduced in a giant photograph nearby; its curved shape resembled an ice ribbon. The original would stay in storage with the collodion plates and the mercury thermometer. The copies offered enough detail for anyone who wanted to look closely.","A visitor asked about the neurotransmitter film. Noor could speak of the research without turning the evening into a clinical consultation. She described why images needed clear labels, then showed the visitor the legend. The diagram would not propagate a claim that one chemical explained an entire human life. The person thanked her and returned to the wall of old portrai
